\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{r<r^{\prime}\in[N_{\ell}]}(N_{\ell}-r^{\prime})^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{2}\mathbb{E}\varphi^{2}_{\ell r\ell r^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{r<r^{\prime}\in[N_{\ell}]}(N_{\ell}-r^{\prime})^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{4}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}N_{\ell}^{4}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{4}^{4}.

(244)

Next we study
S_{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\varphi_{\ell r\ell r}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}\delta_{j_{1}j_{2}\ell}\delta_{j_{3}j_{4}\ell}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}Z_{\ell j_{2}r}Z_{\ell j_{3}r}Z_{\ell j_{4}r}.

We have the following bounds by casework.

\displaystyle|\delta_{j_{1}j_{2}\ell}\delta_{j_{3}j_{4}\ell}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}Z_{\ell j_{2}r}Z_{\ell j_{3}r}Z_{\ell j_{4}r}|

(245)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\delta_{jj\ell}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell jr}^{4}\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j}^{3}\hskip 8.50012pt&\text{ if }j_{1}=\cdots=j_{4}\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{1}\ell}\delta_{j_{1}j_{4}\ell}|\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}^{3}Z_{\ell j_{4}r}|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{3}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}^{2}&\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3},j_{1}\neq j_{4}\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{1}\ell}\delta_{j_{3}j_{3}\ell}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{3}r}^{2}\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}^{2}&\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2},j_{3}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3}\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{2}\ell}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{2}r}^{2}\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{3}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}^{3}&\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{3},j_{2}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3}\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{1}\ell}\delta_{j_{3}j_{4}\ell}|\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{3}r}Z_{\ell j_{4}r}|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}^{2}&\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2},j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{2}\ell}\delta_{j_{1}j_{4}\ell}|\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{2}r}Z_{\ell j_{4}}|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{3}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}^{2}&\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{3},j_{1},j_{2},j_{4}\,\,dist.\\
\delta_{j_{1}j_{2}\ell}\delta_{j_{3}j_{4}\ell}|\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r}Z_{\ell j_{2}r}Z_{\ell j_{3}r}Z_{\ell j_{4}r}|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}^{2}&\text{ if }j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.\end{cases}

Up to symmetry, this accounts for all possible (nonzero) cases. Hence by Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\varphi_{\ell r\ell r}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{2}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{4}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{6}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{6}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{4}+\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{8}\lesssim\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}.

(246)

Recalling that
\{\varphi_{\ell r\ell r}\}_{\ell,r\in[N_{\ell}]}
is an uncorrelated collection of random variables, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(S_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{r\in[N_{\ell}]}(N_{\ell}-r)^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{\ell r\ell r}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\ell}\sum_{r\in[N_{\ell}]}(N_{\ell}-r)^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}N_{\ell}^{3}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}.

(247)

Combining (
247
) and (
244
) proves the result.
∎

E.7
Proof of Lemma
19

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}E_{\ell,s}^{4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4}\in[s-1]}\,\,\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{1}s}Z_{\ell j_{2}r_{2}}Z_{\ell j_{2}s}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{3}s}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{4}}Z_{\ell j_{4}s}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}\bigg[\,\,\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}s}Z_{\ell j_{2}s}Z_{\ell j_{3}s}Z_{\ell j_{4}s}]\cdot\underbrace{\sum_{r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4}\in[s-1]}\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{2}r_{2}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{4}}]}_{=:B_{\ell,s;j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}}\,\,\bigg]

(248)

We have by exhaustive casework that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{2}r_{2}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{4}}]|

(249)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{4}\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3}=j_{4};\\
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=r_{4}\end{subarray}\\
\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{3}}^{2}\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3}=j_{4};\\
r_{1}=r_{2},r_{3}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{3}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{3}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{1}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3},j_{1}\neq j_{4};\\
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=r_{4}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{3}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3},j_{1}\neq j_{4};\\
r_{1}=r_{2},r_{3}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{3}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{1}}^{2}|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{3}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3};\\
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=r_{4}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}^{2}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{3}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3};\\
r_{1}=r_{2},r_{3}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{3}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{1}}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{2}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{2}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}^{2}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{3}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3};\\
r_{1}=r_{3},r_{2}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{2}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{1}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.;\\
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=r_{4}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}^{2}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{3}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.;\\
r_{1}=r_{2},r_{3}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{3}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{1}}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{2}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{2}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1}=j_{2},j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.;\\
r_{1}=r_{3},r_{2}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{2}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{2}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{1}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist;\\
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=r_{4}\end{subarray}\\
|\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell j_{1}r_{1}}Z_{\ell j_{2}r_{1}}\mathbb{E}Z_{\ell j_{3}r_{3}}Z_{\ell j_{4}r_{3}}]|\lesssim\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\text{ if }\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist;\\
r_{1}=r_{2},r_{3}=r_{4},r_{1}\neq r_{3}\end{subarray}\end{cases}

Up to permutation of the indices
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}
and
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4}
, this accounts for all possible cases such that (
249
) is nonzero. Therefore,

\displaystyle B_{\ell,s;j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3}=j_{4}\\
s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2}=j_{3},j_{1}\neq j_{4}\\
s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2},j_{3}=j_{4},j_{1}\neq j_{3}\\
s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }j_{1}=j_{2},j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.\\
s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\,\,dist.\\
\end{cases}

Up to permutation of
j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}
, this accounts for all possible cases. Returning to (
248
), we have by applying (
219
) and the previous display that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}E_{\ell,s}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\bigg(\sum_{j}\Omega_{\ell j}(s\Omega_{\ell j}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j}^{2})+\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{4}}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}(s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}})

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}(s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}})

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{j_{1},j_{3},j_{4}(dist.)}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}(s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}})

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}\,dist.}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}(s\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}}+s^{2}\Omega_{\ell j_{1}}\Omega_{\ell j_{2}}\Omega_{\ell j_{3}}\Omega_{\ell j_{4}})\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim s\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{2}+s^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}.

In the third line we group the coefficients of
s
and
s^{2}
and use the fact that
\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{4}\leq\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}
by Cauchy–Schwarz. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{(\ell,s)}\mathbb{E}E_{\ell,s}^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{(\ell,s)}s\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{2}+\sum_{(\ell,s)}s^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}\sum_{s\in[N_{\ell}]}s\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{2}+\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}\sum_{s\in[N_{\ell}]}s^{2}\sigma_{\ell}^{4}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}N_{\ell}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|^{2}+\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell\in S_{k}}N_{\ell}^{3}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\|\Omega_{\ell}\|_{3}^{3},

as desired.
∎

E.8
Proof of Lemma
20

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\frac{N_{i}^{2}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k}\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\sum_{i,m\in S_{k}}N_{i}N_{m}\langle\Omega_{i},\Omega_{m}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k}\frac{1}{n_{k}^{2}\bar{N}_{k}^{2}}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2},

which establishes the first claim.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\frac{N_{i}^{3}\|\Omega_{i}\|_{3}^{3}}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k}\frac{1}{n_{k}^{4}\bar{N}_{k}^{4}}\sum_{i,m,m^{\prime}\in S_{k}}N_{i}N_{m}N_{m^{\prime}}\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{m^{\prime}j}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k}\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}\|\mu_{k}\|_{3}^{3},

which proves the second claim.

The third claim follows similarly and we omit the proof.

∎

Appendix F
Proofs of other main lemmas and theorems

F.1
Proof of Lemma
1

We start from computing
\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\mu}_{kj}-\hat{\mu}_{j})^{2}]
.
Write
X_{ij}=N_{i}(\Omega_{ij}+Y_{ij})
. It follows by elementary calculation that

\hat{\mu}_{kj}-\hat{\mu}_{j}=\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}Y_{ij}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\sum_{\ell:\ell\neq k}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}Y_{ij}.

For different
k
, the variables
\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}Y_{ij}
are independent of each other.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\mu}_{kj}

\displaystyle-\hat{\mu}_{j})^{2}]=(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)^{2}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}Y_{ij}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr]+\sum_{\ell:\ell\neq k}\frac{1}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl(\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}Y_{ij}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr]

(250)

\displaystyle=(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}+\Bigl(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)^{2}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})+\sum_{\ell:\ell\neq k}\frac{1}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})

(251)

\displaystyle=(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{k}^{2}\bar{N}_{k}^{2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})

(252)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt+\frac{1}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\biggl[\Bigl(1-\frac{n\bar{N}}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}\Bigr)\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})+\sum_{\ell:\ell\neq k}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})\biggr]

(253)

\displaystyle=(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}+\frac{1}{n_{k}^{2}\bar{N}_{k}^{2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})

(254)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}\underbrace{\biggl[\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})-\frac{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})\biggr]}_{\delta_{kj}}.

(255)

Since
X_{ij}
follows a binomial distribution, it is easy to see that
\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}]=N_{i}\Omega_{ij}
and
\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}^{2}]=(\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}])^{2}+\mathrm{Var}(X_{ij})=N_{i}^{2}\Omega^{2}_{ij}+N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})
. Combining them gives

\mathbb{E}[X_{ij}(N_{i}-X_{ij})]=N_{i}(N_{i}-1)\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij}).

(256)

Define

\hat{\zeta}_{kj}=(\hat{\mu}_{kj}-\hat{\mu}_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{k}^{2}\bar{N}_{k}^{2}}\Bigl(1-\frac{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\frac{X_{ij}(N_{i}-X_{ij})}{N_{i}-1},

It follows from (
250
)-(
256
) that

\mathbb{E}[\hat{\zeta}_{kj}]=(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}\delta_{kj}.

(257)

We are ready to compute
\mathbb{E}[T]
. By definition,
T=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}\hat{\zeta}_{kj}
and
\rho^{2}=\sum_{j,k}(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}
. Consequently,

\mathbb{E}[T]=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}\Bigl[(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}\delta_{kj}\Bigr]=\rho^{2}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\delta_{kj}.

(258)

We use the definition of
\delta_{kj}
in (
250
). It is seen that for each
1\leq j\leq p
,

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\delta_{kj}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})-\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}\Omega_{ij}(1-\Omega_{ij})=0.

(259)

Combining (
258
)-(
259
) gives
\mathbb{E}[T]=\rho^{2}
. This proves the claim. ∎

F.2
Proof of Theorem
3

First we show that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(T)\lesssim\Theta_{n}

(260)

Recall

\displaystyle\Theta_{n1}

\displaystyle=4\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{p}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}\mu_{kj}

\displaystyle\Theta_{n2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Bigl(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)^{2}\frac{N_{i}^{3}}{N_{i}-1}\Omega_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle\Theta_{n3}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{1\leq k\neq\ell\leq K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}

\displaystyle\Theta_{n4}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in S_{k},m\in S_{k},\\
i\neq m\end{subarray}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\Bigl(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}\Bigr)^{2}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}.

and that
\sum_{a=1}^{4}\Theta_{na}=\Theta_{n}
.

By Lemma
4
, we immediately have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{1})\leq\Theta_{n1}.

(261)

For
U_{2}
, it is shown in the Proof of Lemma
5
that

\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2})=4\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq N_{i}}\frac{\theta_{i}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}\bigl[\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}+O(\|\Omega_{i}\|_{3}^{3})].

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim 4\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq N_{i}}\frac{\theta_{i}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\theta_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}=\Theta_{n2}

(262)

Next we study
U_{3}
. Using that
\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}\leq 1
and
\|\Omega_{i}\|_{1}=1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq\ell}\frac{n_{k}n_{\ell}\bar{N}_{k}\bar{N}_{\ell}}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}{\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{k}\circ\Sigma_{\ell}){\bf 1}_{p}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{k\neq\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{k\neq\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}\sum_{j^{\prime}}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{k\neq\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}.

Therefore by Lemma
6
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\mathbf{1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{3})\lesssim\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{1\leq k\neq\ell\leq K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}N_{i}N_{m}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}=\Theta_{n3}.

(263)

Similarly for
U_{4}
, we have by the Proof of Lemma
7
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{4})

\displaystyle=4\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in S_{k},m\in S_{k}\\
i<m\end{subarray}}\kappa_{im}\Bigl(\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}+\delta_{im}\Bigr)

(264)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in S_{k},m\in S_{k}\\
i<m\end{subarray}}\kappa_{im}\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}=\Theta_{n4}.

(265)

Above we use that
|\delta_{im}|\leq\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}
and recall that
\kappa_{im}=(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}})^{2}N_{i}N_{m}
.

Observe that by Lemma
1
,

\displaystyle\Theta_{n1}=4\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{j=1}^{p}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}(\mu_{kj}-\mu_{j})^{2}\mu_{kj}\lesssim\max_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\rho^{2}=\max_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\mathbb{E}\,T.

(266)

Since (
23
) holds, Lemma
8
applies and

\displaystyle\Theta_{n_{2}}+\Theta_{n3}+\Theta_{n4}\asymp\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}.

(267)

Combining (
260
), (
266
), and (
267
) proves the theorem.
∎

F.3
Proof of Theorem
4

To prove Theorem
4
, we must prove the following claims:

(a)

Under the alternative hypothesis,
\psi\to\infty
in probability.

(b)

For any fixed
\kappa\in(0,1)
, the level-
\kappa
DELVE test has an asymptotic level of
\kappa
and an asymptotic power of
1
.

(c)

If we choose
\kappa=\kappa_{n}
such that
\kappa_{n}\to 0
and
1-\Phi(\mathrm{SNR}_{n})=o(\kappa_{n})
, where
\Phi
is the CDF of
N(0,1)
, then the sum of type I and type II errors of the DELVE test converges to
0
.

We show the first claim, that
\psi\to\infty
, under the alternative hypothesis and the conditions of Theorem
4
. In particular, recall we assume that

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}=\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}\to\infty.

(268)

Our first goal is to show that

\displaystyle T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\to}}\infty

(269)

under the alternative.
By Chebyshev’s inequality, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,T\gg\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}.

(270)

By Theorem
3
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(T)\lesssim\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}+\max_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\mathbb{E}T=\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}+\max_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}\cdot\rho^{2}

(271)

By (
268
),

\mathbb{E}T=\rho^{2}\gg\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}\geq\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\|\mu_{k}\|_{\infty}}\cdot\rho\ll\rho^{2}=\mathbb{E}T.

(272)

Moreover, by (
268
),

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}\ll\rho^{4}=(\mathbb{E}T)^{2}.

(273)

Combining (
271
), (
272
), and (
273
) implies (
269
).

Next we show that
V>0
with high probability (i.e., with probability tending to
1
as
n\bar{N}\to\infty
). Recall that by Lemmas
8
,
12
, and
13
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}V

\displaystyle=\Theta_{n2}+\Theta_{n3}+\Theta_{n4}\gtrsim\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}>0,\,\text{ and}

(274)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(V)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k}\frac{\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}{n_{k}^{2}\bar{N}_{k}^{2}}\vee\sum_{k}\frac{\|\mu_{k}\|_{3}^{3}}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}.

(275)

Using this, the Markov inequality, and (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(V<\mathbb{E}[V]/2\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(|V-\mathbb{E}[V]|\geq\mathbb{E}[V]/2\bigr)\leq\frac{4\mathrm{Var}(V)}{(\mathbb{E}[V])^{2}}=o(1),

(276)

which implies that
V>0
with high probability.

To finish the proof of the first claim, note that the assumptions of Proposition
2
are satisfied and we have
V/\mathrm{Var}(T)=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. By this, (
269
), and (
276
), we have

\displaystyle\psi=\frac{T{\bf 1}_{V>0}}{\sqrt{V}}=\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}}{\sqrt{V}}\cdot\frac{T}{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}}\cdot{\bf 1}_{V>0}\gtrsim\frac{T}{\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}}\to\infty

in probability.

The second claim follows directly from the first claim and Theorem
2
.

To prove the third claim, by Chebyshev’s inequality and
T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\to\infty
, it follows that
T>(1/2)\mathbb{E}T=(1/2)\rho^{2}
with high probability as
n\bar{N}\to\infty
. By a similar Chebyshev argument as above, it also holds that
V<(3/2)\mathbb{E}V
with high probability as
n\bar{N}\to\infty
. Recall that
\mathbb{E}V=\Theta_{n2}+\Theta_{n3}+\Theta_{n4}\lesssim\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}
by Lemmas
8
and
12
. Thus, with high probability as
n\bar{N}\to\infty
, we have

\psi=T{\bf 1}_{V>0}/\sqrt{V}\gtrsim\rho^{2}/\sqrt{\mathbb{E}V}\gtrsim\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}=\mathrm{SNR}_{n}.

Choosing
\alpha_{n}
as specified yield the third claim. The proof is complete since all three claims are established.

∎

F.4
Proof of Theorem
5

Without loss of generality,
we assume
p
is even and write
m=p/2
. Let
\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a nonnegative vector with
\|\mu\|_{1}=1/2

. Let
\tilde{\mu}=(\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We consider the null hypothesis:

H_{0}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{i}=\tilde{\mu},\hskip 17.00024pt1\leq i\leq n.

(277)

We pair it with a random alternative hypothesis. Let
b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{m}
be a collection of i.i.d. Rademacher variables. Let
z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{K}
denote an independent collection of i.i.d. Rademacher random variables conditioned on the event

|\sum_{k}z_{k}|\leq 100\sqrt{K}.

For a properly small sequence
\omega_{n}>0
of positive numbers, let

H_{1}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{ij}=\begin{cases}\mu_{j}\bigl(1+\omega_{n}(n_{k}\bar{N}_{k})^{-1}\big(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in K}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}\big)z_{k}b_{j}\bigr),&\mbox{if }1\leq j\leq m,i\in S_{k}\cr\tilde{\mu}_{j}\bigl(1-\omega_{n}(n_{k}\bar{N}_{k})^{-1}\big(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in K}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}\big)z_{k}b_{j-m}\bigr),&\mbox{if }m+1\leq j\leq 2m,i\in S_{k}\end{cases}

(278)

In this section we slightly abuse notation, using
\omega_{n}
to refer to the (deterministic) sequence above and reserving
\omega(\Omega)
for the random quantity

\omega(\Omega)=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}\|\mu_{k}-\mu\|^{2}}.

(279)

As long as

\omega_{n}\leq\frac{\min_{k}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in[K]}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}=\frac{\min_{k}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}{n\bar{N}/K},

then
\Omega_{ij}\geq 0
for all
i\in[n],j\in[p]
. Furthermore, for each
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\|\Omega_{i}\|_{1}=2\|\mu\|_{1}=1
. We suppose there exists a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle cK^{-1}n\bar{N}\leq n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq c^{-1}K^{-1}n\bar{N}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{for all }k\in[K]

(280)

With (
280
) in hand, we may assume without loss of generality that

\displaystyle\omega_{n}\leq c/2

(281)

This assumption implies that (
278
) is well-defined and moreover
\Omega_{ij}\asymp\mu_{j}
.

Next we characterize the random quantity
\omega(\Omega)
in terms of
\omega_{n}
.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
\omega^{2}(\Omega)
be as in (
279
). When
\Omega
follows Model (
278
), there exists a constant
c_{1}\in(0,1)
such that
c_{1}\omega_{n}^{2}\leq\omega^{2}(\Omega)\leq c_{1}^{-1}\omega_{n}^{2}
with probability
1
.

The proof of Lemma
F.1
is given in Section
F.4.1
. By Lemma
F.1
, under the model (
278
) it holds with probability
1
that

\displaystyle\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega^{2}(\Omega)}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}\asymp K^{-1/2}n\bar{N}\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}.

(282)

Above we use that
\Omega_{ij}\asymp\mu_{j}
, since we assume (
281
)

We also require Proposition
F.2
below, whose proof is given in Section
F.4.2
.

Proposition F.2
.

Suppose that (
280
) and (
281
) hold. Consider the pair of hypotheses in (
277
)-(
278
) and let
\mathbb{P}_{0}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{1}
be the respective probability measures. If

\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega^{2}(\Omega)}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}\asymp K^{-1/2}n\bar{N}\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}\to 0,

then the chi-square distance between
\mathbb{P}_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1}
converges to
0
.

Now we prove Theorem
5
. Let
\delta_{n}
denote an arbitrary sequence tending to
0
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\delta_{n}\leq c^{*}
for a small absolute constant
c^{*}\in(0,1)
. Note that
K^{-1/2}n\bar{N}\geq 1
since
K\leq n
. Thus for appropriate choice of sequences of
\mu=\mu_{n}
and
\omega_{n}\leq c/2
in models (
277
), (
278
) and applying (
282
), we obtain

\displaystyle 2\delta_{n}\geq\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega^{2}(\Omega)}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}}\geq\delta_{n}.

(283)

Recall the definitions of
\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{0n}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{1n}
in (
30
). Let
\Pi
denote the distribution on
\xi=\{(N_{i},\Omega_{i},\ell_{i})\}\in\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{1n}
induced by (
278
). Let
\xi_{0}
denote the parameter associated to the simple null hypothesis in (
277
) associated to our choice of
\mu
and
\omega_{n}
satisfying (
283
). We have by standard manipulations,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{0n},\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{1n})

\displaystyle:=\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\sup_{\xi\in{\cal Q}_{0n}^{*}(c_{0},\epsilon_{n})}\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\Psi=1)+\sup_{\xi\in{\cal Q}_{1n}^{*}(\delta_{n};c_{0},\epsilon_{n})}\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\Psi=0)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\sup_{\xi\in{\cal Q}_{0n}^{*}(c_{0},\epsilon_{n}),\xi^{\prime}\in{\cal Q}_{1n}^{*}(\delta_{n};c_{0},\epsilon_{n})}\big[\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\Psi=1)+\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\Psi=0)\big]

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\sup_{\xi\in{\cal Q}_{0n}^{*}(c_{0},\epsilon_{n})}\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\prime}\sim\Pi}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\xi}(\Psi=1)+\mathbb{P}_{\xi^{\prime}}(\Psi=0)\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\prime}\sim\Pi}\bigg[\mathbb{P}_{\xi_{0}}(\Psi=1)+\mathbb{P}_{\xi^{\prime}}(\Psi=0)\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Psi=1)+\mathbb{P}_{1}(\Psi=0)\bigg\}.

In the last line we recall the definition of
\mathbb{P}_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1}
in (
277
) and (
278
), noting that for all events
E
,

\mathbb{P}_{1}(E)=\mathbb{E}_{\xi^{\prime}\sim\pi}\,\mathbb{P}_{\xi^{\prime}}(E).

Next, by the Neyman–Pearson lemma and the standard inequality
\text{TV}(P,Q)\leq\sqrt{\chi^{2}(P,Q)}
(see e.g. Chapter 2 of
tsybakov2009introduction
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{0n},\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{1n})

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\Psi\in\{0,1\}}\Bigl\{\mathbb{P}_{0}(\Psi=1)+\mathbb{P}_{1}(\Psi=0)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=1-\text{TV}\big(\mathbb{P}_{0},\mathbb{P}_{1}\big)\geq 1-\sqrt{\chi^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{0},\mathbb{P}_{1})}.

By Proposition
F.2
, as
\delta_{n}\to 0
we have
\chi^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{0},\mathbb{P}_{1})\to 0
and thus
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{0n},\mathcal{Q}^{*}_{1n})\to 1
, as desired.

∎

F.4.1
Proof of Proposition
F.1

Next, we perform a change of parameters that preserves the signal strength and chi-squared distance. The testing problem (
277
) and (
278
) has parameters
\Omega_{ij},N_{i},\bar{N}_{k},n_{k},n,
and
K
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{0}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1}
denote the distributions corresponding to the null and alternative hypotheses, respectively. For each
k\in[K]
, we combine all documents in sample
k
to obtain new null and alternative distributions
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{0}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}
with parameters
\tilde{\Omega}_{ij},\tilde{N}_{i},\bar{\tilde{N}}_{i},\tilde{n}_{i},\tilde{n},
and
\tilde{K}
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{K}

\displaystyle=K=\tilde{n}

\displaystyle\tilde{N}_{i}

\displaystyle=n_{i}\bar{N}_{i}

for
i\in[\tilde{K}]

\displaystyle\bar{\tilde{N}}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\tilde{N}_{i}

for
i\in[\tilde{K}]

\displaystyle\tilde{n}_{i}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ for $i\in[\tilde{K}]$ }.

(284)

For notational ease, we define
\tilde{N}:=\bar{\tilde{N}}=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\in[K]}n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}
. Furthermore, we have
\tilde{\Omega}_{i}=\mu
for all
i\in[\tilde{n}]
under the null
\tilde{\Omega}_{i}=\mu_{i}
for all
i\in[\tilde{n}]
under the alternative. Explicitly, in the reparameterized model, we have the null hypothesis

H_{0}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{i}=\tilde{\mu},\hskip 17.00024pt1\leq i\leq n.

(285)

and alternative hypothesis

H_{1}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{ij}=\begin{cases}\mu_{j}\bigl(1+\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z_{i}b_{j}\bigr),&\mbox{if }1\leq j\leq m,\cr\tilde{\mu}_{j}\bigl(1-\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z_{i}b_{j-m}\bigr),&\mbox{if }m+1\leq j\leq 2m.\end{cases}

(286)

for all
i\in[\tilde{K}]=[K]=[\tilde{n}]
. Observe that the likelihood ratio is preserved:
\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}{d\mathbb{P}_{1}}=\frac{\tilde{d}\mathbb{P}_{0}}{d\tilde{\mathbb{P}}_{1}}
and also
\omega(\Omega)=\omega(\widetilde{\Omega})
. For simplicity we work with this reparameterized model in this proof.

If
z_{1},\ldots,z_{\tilde{n}}
are independent Rademacher random variables then with probability at least
1/2
it holds that

\displaystyle|\sum_{i}z_{i}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}}

(287)

by Hoeffding’s inequality. Recall that our random model is defined in (
278
) where (i)
z_{1},\ldots,z_{\tilde{n}}
are independent Rademacher random variables conditioned on the event
|\sum_{i}z_{i}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}}
, and (ii)
b_{1},\ldots,b_{m}
are independent Rademacher random variables.

Now we study
\omega^{2}(\widetilde{\Omega})
. For each
1\leq j\leq m
, we have
\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}=\mu_{j}(1+\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z_{i}b_{j})
. Define
\eta_{j}=(\tilde{n}\tilde{N})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}=\mu_{j}(1+\omega_{n}\bar{z}b_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq m
and

\eta_{j}=(\tilde{n}\tilde{N})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}=\tilde{\mu}_{j}(1-\omega_{n}\bar{z}b_{j})
for
m<j\leq 2m
.
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{N}_{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}-\eta_{j})^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{N}_{i}\cdot\mu_{j}^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}\frac{\tilde{N}^{2}}{\tilde{N}_{i}^{2}}(z_{i}-\bar{z})^{2}b_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\omega_{n}^{2}\tilde{N}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}(z_{i}-\bar{z})^{2}.

By (
287
),
|\bar{z}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}}
. Thus
|z_{i}-\bar{z}|\asymp 1
. Write
\tilde{N}_{*}=(\tilde{n}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1})
. It follows that

\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{N}_{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}-\eta_{j})^{2}\asymp\omega_{n}^{2}\tilde{N}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{N}_{*}^{-1}.

Note that
\tilde{N}\geq\tilde{N}_{*}
. Additionally, by assumption (
280
),
\tilde{N}_{i}\asymp\tilde{N}\leq c^{-1}\tilde{N}_{*}
. It follows that

\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{N}_{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}-\eta_{j})^{2}\asymp\tilde{n}\tilde{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}.

(288)

Moreover,
\|\eta\|^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mu_{j}^{2}(1+\omega_{n}\bar{z}b_{j})^{2}
. By our conditioning on the event in (
287
),

|\omega_{n}\bar{z}b_{j}|\lesssim\omega_{n}{\tilde{n}}^{-1/2}.

Since
\omega_{n}\leq 1
and
\sum_{j}b_{j}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\eta\|^{2}=\|\mu\|^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mu_{j}^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}\bar{z}^{2}=\|\mu\|^{2}[1+O(\tilde{n}^{-1})]\asymp\|\mu\|^{2}.

(289)

Hence

\omega^{2}(\widetilde{\Omega})=\omega^{2}(\Omega)\asymp\omega_{n}^{2},\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{where recall}\hskip 8.50012pt\omega(\widetilde{\Omega})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{N}_{i}(\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}-\eta_{j})^{2}}{{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}\|\eta\|^{2}}.

(290)

This finishes the proof.
∎

F.4.2
Proof of Proposition
F.2

In this proof, we continue to employ the reparametrization in (
284
). As discussed there, this reparametrization preserves the likelihood ratio and thus the chi-square distance.

By definition,
\chi^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{0},\mathbb{P}_{1})=\int(\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{1}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}})^{2}d\mathbb{P}_{0}-1
.
It suffices to show that

\int\Bigl(\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{1}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}\Bigr)^{2}d\mathbb{P}_{0}=1+o(1).

(291)

From the density of of multinomial distribution,
d\mathbb{P}_{0}=\prod_{i,j}\tilde{\mu}_{j}^{X_{ij}}
, and
d\mathbb{P}_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{b,z}[\prod_{i,j}\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}^{X_{ij}}]
.
It follows that

\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{1}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}=\mathbb{E}_{b,z}\biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\Bigl(\frac{\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}}{\tilde{\mu}_{j}}\Bigr)^{X_{ij}}\biggr].

Let
b^{(0)}=(b^{(0)}_{1},\ldots,b^{(0)}_{m})^{\prime}
and
z^{(0)}=(z^{(0)}_{1},\cdots,z^{(0)}_{\tilde{n}})^{\prime}
be independent copies of
b
and
z
. We construct
\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}
similarly as in (
286
). It is seen that

\displaystyle\int\Bigl(\frac{d\mathbb{P}_{1}}{d\mathbb{P}_{0}}\Bigr)^{2}d\mathbb{P}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X}\mathbb{E}_{b,z,b^{(0)},z^{(0)}}\biggl[\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\Bigl(\frac{\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}}{\tilde{\mu}^{2}_{j}}\Bigr)^{X_{ij}}\biggr]

(292)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{b,z,b^{(0)},z^{(0)}}\biggl\{\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{i}}\biggl[\prod_{j=1}^{p}\Bigl(\frac{\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}}{\tilde{\mu}^{2}_{j}}\Bigr)^{X_{ij}}\biggr]\biggr\}

(293)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{b,z,b^{(0)},z^{(0)}}\biggl\{\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\biggl(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{\mu}_{j}\cdot\frac{\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}}{\tilde{\mu}_{j}^{2}}\Bigr)^{\tilde{N}_{i}}\biggr]\biggr\}

(294)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\exp(M)],\hskip 8.50012pt\mbox{with}\hskip 8.50012ptM:=\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}\log\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{\mu}_{j}^{-1}\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}\Bigr).

(295)

Here, the third line follows from the moment generating function of a multinomial distribution. We plug in the expression of
\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}
in (
278
). By direct calculations,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\tilde{\mu}_{j}^{-1}\widetilde{\Omega}_{ij}\widetilde{\Omega}^{(0)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}\bigl(1+\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z_{i}b_{j}\bigr)\bigl(1+\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z^{(0)}_{i}b^{(0)}_{j}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.00024pt+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}\bigl(1-\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z_{i}b_{j}\bigr)\bigl(1-\omega_{n}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N}z^{(0)}_{i}b^{(0)}_{j}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\|\mu\|_{1}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}\omega_{n}^{2}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-2}\tilde{N}^{2}z_{i}z^{(0)}_{i}b_{j}b^{(0)}_{j}

\displaystyle=1+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}\omega_{n}^{2}\tilde{N}_{i}^{-2}\tilde{N}^{2}z_{i}z^{(0)}_{i}b_{j}b^{(0)}_{j}.

We plug it into
M
and notice that
\log(1+t)\leq t
is always true. It follows that

M\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\tilde{N}_{i}\cdot 2\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}\omega_{n}^{2}\frac{\tilde{N}^{2}}{\tilde{N}_{i}^{2}}z_{i}z^{(0)}_{i}b_{j}b^{(0)}_{j}=2\tilde{N}\omega_{n}^{2}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}\frac{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{N}_{i}}z_{i}z^{(0)}_{i}\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mu_{j}b_{j}b^{(0)}_{j}\Bigr)=:M^{*}.

(296)

We combine (
296
) with (
292
). It is seen that to show (
291
), it suffices to show that

\mathbb{E}[\exp(M^{*})]=1+o(1).

(297)

We now show (
297
). Write
M_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}(\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N})z_{i}z^{(0)}_{i}
and
M_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mu_{j}b_{j}b^{(0)}_{j}
.

Recall that we condition on the event (
287
). By Hoeffding’s inequality, Bayes’s rule, and (
287
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|M_{1}|>t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(|\sum_{i}\frac{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{N}_{i}}z_{i}z_{i}^{(0)}\geq t\,\,\bigg|\,\,|\sum_{i}z_{i}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}},|\sum_{i}z_{i}^{(0)}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}\big(|\sum_{i}\frac{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{N}_{i}}z_{i}z_{i}^{(0)}|\geq t\big)}{\mathbb{P}(|\sum_{i}z_{i}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}})\,\,\mathbb{P}(|\sum_{i}z_{i}^{(0)}|\leq 100\sqrt{\tilde{n}})}

\displaystyle\leq 4\cdot 2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{8\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{n}}(\tilde{N}_{i}^{-1}\tilde{N})^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=8\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{8{\tilde{n}}}\Bigr).

for all
t>0
. In the last line, we have used the assumption of
\tilde{N}_{i}\asymp\tilde{N}
.
By Hoeffding’s inequality again, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|M_{2}|>t)\leq 2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{8\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mu_{j}^{2}}\Bigr)=2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{8\|\mu\|^{2}}\Bigr)

for all
t>0
.
Write
s^{2}_{\tilde{n}}=\sqrt{{\tilde{n}}}\tilde{N}\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(M^{*}>t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl(2\tilde{N}\omega_{n}^{2}M_{1}M_{2}>t\bigr)=\mathbb{P}\bigl(M_{1}M_{2}>t\cdot\sqrt{{\tilde{n}}}\|\mu\|s_{\tilde{n}}^{-2}\bigr)

(298)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(M_{1}>\sqrt{t}\cdot\sqrt{{\tilde{n}}}s_{\tilde{n}}^{-1}\bigr)+\mathbb{P}\bigl(M_{2}>\sqrt{t}\cdot\|\mu\|s_{\tilde{n}}^{-1}\bigr)

(299)

\displaystyle\leq 8\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t}{8s^{2}_{\tilde{n}}}\Bigr)+2\exp\Bigl(-\frac{t}{8s_{\tilde{n}}^{2}}\Bigr)

(300)

\displaystyle\leq 4\exp(-c_{1}t/s_{\tilde{n}}^{2}),

(301)

for some constant
c_{1}>0
. Here, in the last line, we have used the assumption of
\tilde{N}_{i}\asymp\tilde{N}
.

Let
f(x)
and
F(x)
be the density and distribution function of
M^{*}
. Write
\bar{F}(x)=1-F(x)
.
Using integration by part, we have
\mathbb{E}[\exp(M^{*})]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(x)f(x)dx=-\exp(x)\bar{F}(x)|_{0}^{\infty}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(x)\bar{F}(x)dx=1+\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(x)\bar{F}(x)dx
, provided that the integral exists. As a result, when
s_{\tilde{n}}=o(1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(M^{*})]-1

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(t)\cdot\mathbb{P}(M^{*}>t)

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\bigl(-[c_{1}s_{\tilde{n}}^{-2}-1]t\bigr)dt

\displaystyle\leq 4(c_{1}s_{\tilde{n}}^{-1}-1)^{-1}=4s_{\tilde{n}}/(c_{1}-s_{\tilde{n}}).

It implies
\mathbb{E}[\exp(M^{*})]=1+o(1)
, which is exactly (
297
). This completes the proof.
because

s^{2}_{\tilde{n}}=\sqrt{{\tilde{n}}}\tilde{N}\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|=\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{K}}\asymp\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k\in K}\|\mu_{k}\|^{2}}.}

∎

F.5
Proof of Theorem
6

First we show that

\displaystyle T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\Rightarrow N(0,1),\,\,\text{ and}

(302)

\displaystyle V/\mathrm{Var}(T)\to 1.

(303)

If (
302
) and (
303
) hold, then by mimicking the proof of Theorem
2
, we see that
\psi
is asymptotically normal and the level-
\kappa
DELVE test has asymptotic level
\kappa
. We omit the details as they are quite similar.

Recall the martingale decomposition of
T
described in Section
E
. Observe that, under our assumptions, Lemmas
15
–
20
are valid. Moreover, by Lemmas
10
and
14

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(T)\gtrsim\Theta_{n2}+\Theta_{n3}+\Theta_{n4}\gtrsim\bigg\|\frac{m\bar{M}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\eta+\frac{n\bar{N}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\theta\bigg\|^{2}.

(304)

Combining (
304
) with Lemmas
15
–
20
and mimicking the argument in Section
E.1
implies that
T/\sqrt{V}\Rightarrow N(0,1)
. Thus (
302
) is established.

Moreover, (
303
) is a direct consequence of our assumptions and Proposition
3
. The claims of Theorem
6
regarding the null hypothesis follow.

To prove the claims about the alternative hypothesis, it suffices to show

\displaystyle T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\to\infty,

(305)

\displaystyle V>0\,\,\text{ with high probability},\,\,\text{and}

(306)

\displaystyle V=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\mathrm{Var}(T)).

(307)

Once these claims are established, we prove that
\psi=T{\bf 1}_{V>0}/\sqrt{V}\to\infty
under the alternative by mimicking the last step of the proof of Theorem
4
in Section
F.3
. We omit the details as they are very similar.

Note that (
307
) follows directly from our assumptions and Proposition
4
.

As in the proof of Theorem
4
in Section
F.3
, to establish (
305
), it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}T=\rho^{2}\gg\mathrm{Var}(T).

(308)

Our main assumption under the alternative when
K=2
is

\frac{\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}}{\big(\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}+\frac{1}{m\bar{M}}\big)\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}}\to\infty.

(309)

As shown in Section
F.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(T)\lesssim\Theta_{n}=\Theta_{n1}+\sum_{t=2}^{4}\Theta_{nt}.

(310)

Applying (
272
) to the first term and Lemma
10
to the remaining terms, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(T)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{\|\eta\|_{\infty},\,\|\theta\|_{\infty}\}\cdot\rho^{2}+\bigg\|\frac{m\bar{M}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\eta+\frac{n\bar{N}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\theta\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}\cdot\rho^{2}+\max\{\|\eta\|^{2},\,\|\theta\|^{2}\}

(311)

Next, note that

\displaystyle\rho^{2}

\displaystyle=n\bar{N}\|\eta-\mu\|^{2}+m\bar{M}\|\theta-\mu\|^{2}

\displaystyle=n\bar{N}\bigg\|\eta-\big(\frac{n\bar{N}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\eta+\frac{m\bar{M}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\theta\big)\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+m\bar{M}\bigg\|\theta-\big(\frac{n\bar{N}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\eta+\frac{m\bar{M}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\theta\big)\bigg\|^{2}

\displaystyle=n\bar{N}\cdot\big(\frac{m\bar{M}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\big)^{2}\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}+m\bar{M}\cdot\big(\frac{n\bar{N}}{n\bar{N}+m\bar{M}}\big)^{2}\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n\bar{N}m\bar{M}}{(n\bar{N}+m\bar{M})}\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}=\big(\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}+\frac{1}{m\bar{M}}\big)^{-1}\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}.

(312)

By (
309
), (
311
), and (
312
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbb{E}T)^{2}}{\mathrm{Var}(T)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\rho^{4}}{\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}\cdot\rho^{2}+\max\{\|\eta\|^{2},\,\|\theta\|^{2}\}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}}{(\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}+\frac{1}{m\bar{M}})\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}}+\big(\frac{\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}}{(\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}+\frac{1}{m\bar{M}})\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}}\big)^{2}\to\infty,

which proves (
308
) and thus (
305
).

To prove (
306
), we mimick the Markov argument in (
276
) and use that under our assumptions,
\mathrm{Var}(V)/(\mathbb{E}V)^{2}=o(1)
. We omit the details as they are similar. Since we have established (
305
), (
306
), and (
307
), the proof is complete.
∎

F.6
Proof of Theorem
7

Note that
T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\Rightarrow N(0,1)
by our assumptions and Proposition
7
. In particular, using that
n\to\infty
and the monotonicity of the
\ell_{p}
norms we have

\frac{\|\mu\|_{4}^{4}}{K\|\mu\|^{4}}=\frac{\|\mu\|_{4}^{4}}{n\|\mu\|^{4}}\leq\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{\|\mu\|^{4}}{\|\mu\|^{4}}=\frac{1}{n}\to 0.

Moreover,
V^{*}/\mathrm{Var}(T)\to 1
in probability by Proposition
5
. It follows by Slutsky’s theorem that
\psi^{*}=T/\sqrt{V^{*}}\Rightarrow N(0,1)
and that the level-
\kappa
DELVE test has an asymptotic level
\kappa
.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to show that
\psi^{*}\to\infty
under the alternative. As in the proof of Theorem
4
, this follows immediately if we can show

\displaystyle T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(T)}\to\infty,

(313)

\displaystyle V^{*}>0\,\,\text{ with high probability},\,\,\text{and}

(314)

\displaystyle V^{*}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\mathrm{Var}(T)).

(315)

Note that (
313
) follows from (
269
), and (
315
) is the content of Proposition
6
. Since our assumptions imply that
\mathbb{E}V^{*}\gg\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(V^{*})}
, (
314
) follows by a Markov argument as in (
276
).

∎

F.7
Proof of Theorem
8

We apply Theorem
2
to get the asymptotic null distribution. Since
N_{i}=N
and
\mu=p^{-1}{\bf 1}_{p}
, it is easy to see that Condition
2
is satisfied under our assumption of
p=o(N^{2}n)
. Therefore, by Theorem
2
,
\psi^{*}\to N(0,1)
under
H_{0}
.

We now show the asymptotic alternative distribution. By direct calculations and using
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{ij}=0
and
\sum_{j=1}^{p}\delta_{ij}=0
, we have

\sum_{i,j}N_{i}(\Omega_{ij}-\mu_{j})^{2}=\frac{nN\nu_{n}^{2}}{p},\hskip 8.50012pt\;\;\sum_{i,j}N_{i}(\Omega_{ij}-\mu_{j})^{2}\Omega_{ij}=\frac{nN\nu_{n}^{2}}{p^{2}},\hskip 8.50012pt\;\;\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}=\frac{n(1+\nu_{n}^{2})}{p}.

We apply Lemmas
3
-
7
and plug in the above expressions. Let
S=\mathbf{1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2}
. It follows that

\displaystyle T=\frac{nN\nu_{n}^{2}}{p}+S+O_{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{nN}\nu_{n}}{p}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\biggr),\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{where}\;\;\mathrm{Var}(S)=2p^{-1}n[1+o(1)].

(316)

First, we plug in
\nu_{n}^{2}=a\sqrt{2p}/(N\sqrt{n})
. It gives
p^{-1}nN\nu_{n}^{2}=\sqrt{2n/p}
. Second,
p^{-1}\sqrt{nN}\nu_{n}\asymp(np)^{-1/4}\sqrt{n/p}=o(\sqrt{n/p})
. It follows that

T=a\sqrt{2n/p}+S+o_{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\sqrt{n/p}\bigr),\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{where}\;\;\mathrm{Var}(S)=(2n/p)[1+o(1)].

(317)

Recall the martingale decomposition
S=\sum_{(\ell,s)}E_{\ell,s}
where
E_{\ell,s}
is defined in (
168
). Observe that Lemmas
18
and
19
hold (even under the alternative). Define
\widetilde{E}_{\ell,s}=E_{\ell,s}/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(S)}
. Using
\mathrm{Var}(S)\gtrsim n\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}
and these lemmas, it is straightforward to verify that the following conditions hold:

\displaystyle\sum_{(\ell,s)}\mathrm{Var}\big(\widetilde{E}_{\ell,s}\big|\mathcal{F}_{\prec(\ell,s)}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\to}}1

(318)

\displaystyle\sum_{(\ell,s)}\mathbb{E}\widetilde{E}_{\ell,s}^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\to}}0.

(319)

As in Section
E.1
, the martingale CLT applies and we have

\displaystyle S/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(S)}\Rightarrow N(0,1).

By
316
,

T/\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(S)}\;\;\to\;\;N(a,1).

(320)

By Lemma
5
and (
160
),

\mathrm{Var}(S)=[1+o(1)]\Theta_{n2}=[1+o(1)]\mathrm{Var}(T)

By Proposition
6
, we have that
V^{*}/\mathrm{Var}(T)\to 1
in probability. As a result,

V^{*}/\mathrm{Var}(S)\;\;\to\;\;1,\hskip 17.00024pt\mbox{in probability}.

(321)

We combine (
320
) and (
321
) to conclude that
\psi=T/\sqrt{V^{*}}\to N(a,1)
.

∎

Appendix G
Proofs of the corollaries for text analysis

G.1
Proof of Corollary
1

Note that Corollary
1
follows immediately from the slightly more general result stated below.

Corollary 9
.

Consider Model (
1
) and suppose that
\Omega=\mu{\bf 1}_{n}^{\prime}
under the null hypothesis and that
\Omega
satisfies (
40
) under the alternative hypothesis. Define
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
by
\xi_{i}=\bar{N}^{-1}N_{i}
and let
\widetilde{\Omega}=\Omega[\mathrm{diag}(\xi)]^{1/2}
. Let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{M}>0
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{M}>0
denote the singular values of
\Omega
and
\widetilde{\Omega}
, respectively, arranged in decreasing order.We further assume that under the alternative hypothesis,

\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}\to\infty.

(322)

For any fixed
\kappa\in(0,1)
, the level-
\kappa
DELVE test has an asymptotic level
\kappa
and an asymptotic power
1
. Moreover if
N_{i}\asymp\bar{N}
for all
i
, we may replace
\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}
with
\sum_{k=2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}
in the numerator of (
322
).

Proof G.1

(Proof of Corollary
9
)
.

This is a special case of our testing problem with
K=n
. Moreover,
\mu=n^{-1}\Omega\xi
matches with the definition of
\mu
in (
2
). Therefore, we can apply Theorem
7
directly. It remains to verify that the condition

\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}\to\infty

(323)

is sufficient to lead to the condition

\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}}\to\infty.

(324)

If we show this then Theorem
7
applies directly.
We first calculate
\omega_{n}^{2}
. Recall
\xi_{i}=N_{i}/\bar{N}
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Write

\widetilde{\Omega}=\Omega[\mathrm{diag}(\xi)]^{1/2},\hskip 17.00024pt\widetilde{\xi}=[\mathrm{diag}(\xi)]^{1/2}{\bf 1}_{n}.

For
K=n
, by (
36
),
\omega_{n}^{2}=\frac{1}{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}N_{i}\|\Omega_{i}-\mu\|^{2}
. It follows that

\omega_{n}^{2}=\frac{1}{n\|\mu\|^{2}}\Bigl\|(\Omega-\mu{\bf 1}^{\prime}_{n})[\mathrm{diag}(\xi)]^{1/2}\Bigr\|_{F}^{2}=\frac{1}{n\|\mu\|^{2}}\bigl\|\widetilde{\Omega}-\mu\widetilde{\xi}^{\prime}\bigr\|_{F}^{2}.

(325)

Recall that
\widetilde{\lambda_{1}},\ldots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{M}
are the singular values of
\widetilde{\Omega}
. We apply a well-known result in linear algebra
(
HornJohnson
)
, namely Weyl’s inequality: For any rank-1 matrix
\Delta
,

\|\widetilde{\Omega}-\Delta\|_{F}^{2}\geq\sum_{k\neq 1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}
.
In (
325
),
\mu\widetilde{\xi}^{\prime}
is a rank-1 matrix.
It follows that

\bigl\|\widetilde{\Omega}-\mu\widetilde{\xi}^{\prime}\bigr\|_{F}^{2}\geq\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}.

(326)

Hence

\displaystyle\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}}\geq\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}{\|\Omega\|_{F}}=\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\widetilde{\lambda}_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}},

which implies (
324
) by our assumption. The first claim is proved.

Next we prove the second claim. Observe that if
N_{i}\asymp\bar{N}
, then by Weyl’s inequality:

\displaystyle\omega_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\|\mu\|^{2}n\bar{N}}\sum_{i}N_{i}\|\Omega_{i}-\mu|^{2}\gtrsim\frac{1}{\|\mu\|^{2}}\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}-\mu\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\|\mu\|^{2}}\|\Omega-\mu{\bf 1}_{n}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{\|\mu\|^{2}}\sum_{k=2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle\frac{n\bar{N}\|\mu\|^{2}\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{i}\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}}\geq\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}{\|\Omega\|_{F}}=\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}.

We see that the assumption

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{N}\cdot\sum_{k=2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}\to\infty

(327)

implies (
324
). The second claim is established and the proof is complete.

G.2
Proof of Corollary
2

Recall the construction of a simple null and simple (random) alternative model from Section
F.4.2
, specialized below to the case of
K=n
and
N_{i}\equiv N
:

H_{0}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{i}=\tilde{\mu},\hskip 17.00024pt1\leq i\leq n.

(328)

H_{1}:\hskip 17.00024pt\Omega_{ij}=\begin{cases}\mu_{j}\bigl(1+\omega_{n}z_{i}b_{j}\bigr),&\mbox{if }1\leq j\leq m\cr\tilde{\mu}_{j}\bigl(1-\omega_{n}z_{i}b_{j-m}\bigr),&\mbox{if }m+1\leq j\leq 2m\end{cases}

(329)

where
b_{1},\ldots,b_{m}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables and
z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}
are i.i.d Rademacher random variables conditioned to satisfy
|\sum_{i}z_{i}|\leq 100\sqrt{n}
. Define

\tilde{b}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{m},b_{1},\ldots,b_{m})^{\prime}.

To derive the lower bound of Corollary
2
, we assume without loss of generality that
\omega_{n}
is a sufficiently small absolute constant.

We claim that
H_{1}
prescribes a topic model with
M=2
topics. To see this,
under the alternative,

\displaystyle\Omega_{i}=\begin{cases}\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}+\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b})&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }z_{i}=1\\
\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}-\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b})&\hskip 8.50012pt\text{ if }z_{i}=-1.\end{cases}

(330)

Moreover, we showed in Section
F.4.2
that
\Omega_{ij}\geq 0
for all
i,j
and that
\|\Omega_{ij}\|_{1}=1
. From (
330
), we see that
\Omega=AW
where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times 2}
and
W\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times n}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle A_{:1}=\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}+\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b}),\hskip 8.50012ptA_{:2}=\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}-\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b})

\displaystyle W_{:i}=\begin{cases}(1,0)^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if }z_{i}=1\\
(0,1)^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\text{if }z_{i}=-1.\end{cases}

Moreover, under the null hypothesis,
\Omega
clearly prescribes a topic model with
K=1
. Therefore
\Omega
follows the topic model (
40
). Moreover, since
N_{i}\equiv N
, we have
\Omega[\mathrm{diag}(\xi)]^{1/2}=\Omega
.

By Proposition
F.2
specialized to our setting, we know that the
\chi^{2}
distance between the null and alternative goes to zero if

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}N\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}\to 0.

Thus to prove Corollary
2
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{N\sum_{k\geq 2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}=\frac{N\lambda_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}\gtrsim\sqrt{n}N\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}

(331)

Accordingly we study the second largest singular value of
\Omega
. First we have some preliminary calculations. Let
U=\{i:z_{i}=1\}
, and let
V=\{i:z_{i}=-1\}
. Define

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}+\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b}),\,\,\text{ and }

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\mu\circ(\mathbf{1}_{p}-\omega_{n}\,\tilde{b}).

Observe that

\displaystyle\langle u,v\rangle

\displaystyle=\|\mu\|^{2}-\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\circ\tilde{b}\|^{2}=\|\mu\|^{2}(1-\omega_{n}^{2}).

Also, since
\omega_{n}
is a sufficiently small absolute constant,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mu\|^{2}+2\omega_{n}\langle\mu,\mu\circ\tilde{b}\rangle+\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\circ\tilde{b}\|^{2}=(1+\omega_{n}^{2})\|\mu\|^{2}+2\omega_{n}\sum_{j}\mu_{j}^{2}\,\tilde{b}_{j}\gtrsim\|\mu\|^{2},\,\,\,\text{ and }

\displaystyle\|v\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\mu\|^{2}-2\omega_{n}\langle\mu,\mu\circ\tilde{b}\rangle+\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\circ\tilde{b}\|^{2}=(1+\omega_{n}^{2})\|\mu\|^{2}-2\omega_{n}\sum_{j}\mu_{j}^{2}\,\tilde{b}_{j}\gtrsim\|\mu\|^{2}.

(332)

Again, since we assume that
\omega_{n}
is a sufficiently small absolute constant,

\displaystyle\delta^{2}:=\frac{\langle u,v\rangle^{2}}{\|u\|^{2}\|v\|^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\mu\|^{4}(1-\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}}{(1+\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}\|\mu\|^{4}-4\omega_{n}^{2}\langle\mu,\mu\circ b\rangle^{2}}\leq\frac{\|\mu\|^{4}(1-\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}}{(1+\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}\|\mu\|^{4}-4\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\mu\|^{4}(1-\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}}{\|\mu\|^{4}(1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4})}=\frac{(1-\omega_{n}^{2})^{2}}{1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4}}

(333)

Note that

\displaystyle\|au+bv\|^{2}

\displaystyle=a^{2}\|u\|^{2}+2ab\langle u,v\rangle+b^{2}\|v\|^{2}\geq a^{2}\|u\|^{2}+b^{2}\|v\|^{2}-2ab\delta\|u\|\|v\|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta)\big(a^{2}\|u\|^{2}+b^{2}\|v\|^{2}\big)+\|au-bv\|^{2}\geq(1-\delta)\big(a^{2}\|u\|^{2}+b^{2}\|v\|^{2}\big).

By (
333
), we have for
\omega_{n}
sufficiently small that

\displaystyle 1-\delta

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1-\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4}}}=\frac{\sqrt{1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4}}-1+\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\omega_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+2\omega_{n}^{2}-3\omega_{n}^{4}}}\gtrsim\omega_{n}^{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle\|au+bv\|^{2}\geq\omega_{n}^{2}(a^{2}\|u\|^{2}+b^{2}\|v\|^{2})\gtrsim\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}(a^{2}+b^{2})

(334)

Recall that if
M
is a rank
k
matrix, then

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}(M)=\sup_{y:\|y\|=1,\,y\in\text{Ker}(M)^{\perp}}\|My\|=\sup_{y:\|y\|=1,\,y\in\text{Im}(M^{\prime})}\|My\|.

(335)

We have

\displaystyle\Omega\Omega^{\prime}=\sum_{i\in U}uu^{\prime}+\sum_{i\in V}vv^{\prime}=|U|uu^{\prime}+|V|vv^{\prime}.

Let
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfy
\|y\|=1
and
y=\Omega^{\prime}x
for some
x
. We have

\displaystyle\Omega y=\Omega\Omega^{\prime}x=|U|\langle u,x\rangle u+|V|\langle v,x\rangle v.

By the previous equation and (
334
),

\displaystyle\|\Omega y\|^{2}=\|\Omega\Omega^{\prime}x\|^{2}=\bigg\||U|\langle u,x\rangle u+|V|\langle v,x\rangle v\bigg\|^{2}\gtrsim\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}\big(|U|^{2}\langle u,x\rangle^{2}+|V|^{2}\langle v,x\rangle^{2}\big).

By our conditioning on
z
, we have
\min(|U|,|V|)\gtrsim n
. Moreover

1=\|y\|^{2}=\|\Omega^{\prime}x\|^{2}=|U|\langle u,x\rangle^{2}+|V|\langle v,x\rangle^{2}.

Applying these facts and (
335
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}^{2}\geq\|\Omega y\|^{2}=\|\Omega\Omega^{\prime}x\|^{2}\gtrsim\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}n\big(|U|\langle u,x\rangle^{2}+|V|\langle v,x\rangle^{2}\big)=\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}n.

Next,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda^{2}_{k}=\|\Omega\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i\in U}\|u\|^{2}+\sum_{i\in V}\|v\|^{2}=|U|\cdot\|u\|^{2}+|V|\cdot\|v\|^{2}\asymp n\|\mu\|^{2}

(336)

We conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{N\sum_{k\geq 2}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}=\frac{N\lambda_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{M}\lambda_{k}^{2}}}\gtrsim\frac{N\cdot\omega_{n}^{2}\|\mu\|^{2}n}{\sqrt{n}\|\mu\|}=\sqrt{n}N\|\mu\|\omega_{n}^{2}

which establishes (
331
). The proof is complete.
∎

G.3
Proof of Corollary
3

This is a special case of our testing problem with
K=2
, we can apply Theorem
6
directly. It remains to verify that the condition

\frac{\zeta_{n}^{2}\cdot(\|\eta_{S}\|_{1}+\|\theta_{S}\|_{1})}{\big(\frac{1}{n\bar{N}}+\frac{1}{m\bar{M}}\big)\max\{\|\eta\|,\,\|\theta\|\}}\to\infty

(337)

is sufficient to yield the condition (
34
) in Theorem
6
. This is done by calculating
\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}
directly.
By our sparse model (
43
), for
j\in S
,
|\sqrt{\eta_{j}}-\sqrt{\theta_{j}}|\geq\zeta_{n}
. It follows that for
j\in S
,

|\eta_{j}-\theta_{j}|^{2}=(\sqrt{\eta_{j}}+\sqrt{\theta_{j}})^{2}(\sqrt{\eta_{j}}-\sqrt{\theta_{j}})^{2}\geq\zeta_{n}^{2}(\sqrt{\eta_{j}}+\sqrt{\theta_{j}})^{2}\geq\zeta_{n}^{2}(\eta_{j}+\theta_{j}).

It follows that

\|\eta-\theta\|^{2}\geq\zeta_{n}^{2}\sum_{j\in S}(\eta_{j}+\theta_{j})\geq\zeta_{n}^{2}\bigl(\|\eta_{S}\|_{1}+\|\theta_{S}\|_{1}\bigr).

(338)

We plug it into (
34
) and see immediately that (
337
) implies this condition. The claim follows directly from Theorem
6
. ∎

Appendix H
A modification of DELVE for finite
p

Below we write out the variance of the terms of the raw DELVE statistic under the null, using the proofs of Lemmas
5
–
7
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq N_{i}}(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}})^{2}\frac{N_{i}^{2}}{(N_{i}-1)^{2}}\big[\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}-2\|\Omega_{i}\|_{3}^{3}+\|\Omega_{i}\|^{4}\big]

(339)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{k\neq\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}N_{m}\Bigl(\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}-2\sum_{j}\Omega^{2}_{ij}\Omega^{2}_{mj}+\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{4})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in S_{k},m\in S_{k}\\
i\neq m\end{subarray}}(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}})^{2}N_{i}N_{m}\Bigl(\sum_{j}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{mj}-2\sum_{j}\Omega^{2}_{ij}\Omega^{2}_{mj}+\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}\Bigr).

In this section we develop an unbiased estimator for each term above, which leads to an unbiased estimator of
\mathrm{Var}(T)
by taking their sum. We require some preliminary results proved later in this section. Recall that Lemma
H.2
was established in the proof of Lemma
3
.

Lemma H.1
.

If
j\neq j^{\prime}
, an unbiased estimator of
\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}}:=\frac{X_{ij}X_{ij^{\prime}}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}

Lemma H.2
.

An unbiased estimator of
\Omega_{ij}^{2}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{2}}:=\frac{X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}.

(340)

Lemma H.3
.

If
j\neq j^{\prime}
, an unbiased estimator for
\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}}=\frac{(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})(X_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}-X_{ij^{\prime}})}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)}

Lemma H.4
.

An unbiased estimator of
\Omega_{ij}^{3}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{3}}:=\frac{X_{ij}^{3}-3X_{ij}^{2}+2X_{ij}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)}.

(341)

Lemma H.5
.

An unbiased estimator of
\Omega_{ij}^{4}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{4}}:=\frac{X_{ij}^{4}-3X_{ij}^{3}-X_{ij}^{2}+3X_{ij}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)}.

(342)

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{2}}

\displaystyle\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|_{3}^{3}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{3}}

\displaystyle\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|^{4}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{4}}+\sum_{j\neq j^{\prime}}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}}.

(343)

Using Lemmas
H.1
–
H.5
and (
343
), we define an unbiased estimator for each term of (
339
). Let
\widehat{\Omega_{ij}}=X_{ij}/N_{i}
and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2})}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq N_{i}}(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}})^{2}\frac{N_{i}^{2}}{(N_{i}-1)^{2}}\big[\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|^{2}}-2\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|_{3}^{3}}+\widehat{\|\Omega_{i}\|^{4}}\big]

(344)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\bar{N}^{2}}\sum_{k\neq\ell}\sum_{i\in S_{k}}\sum_{m\in S_{\ell}}N_{i}N_{m}\Bigl(\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}}\widehat{\Omega_{mj}}-2\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega^{2}_{ij}}\widehat{\Omega^{2}_{mj}}+\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}}\widehat{\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{4})}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in S_{k},m\in S_{k}\\
i\neq m\end{subarray}}(\frac{1}{n_{k}\bar{N}_{k}}-\frac{1}{n\bar{N}})^{2}N_{i}N_{m}\Bigl(\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}}\widehat{\Omega_{mj}}-2\sum_{j}\widehat{\Omega^{2}_{ij}}\widehat{\Omega^{2}_{mj}}+\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}\widehat{\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}}\widehat{\Omega_{mj}\Omega_{mj^{\prime}}}\Bigr).

Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}=\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{2})}+\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{3})}+\widehat{\mathrm{Var}({\bf 1}_{p}^{\prime}U_{4})}.

(345)

We define
exact DELVE
as
\tilde{\psi}=T/\widetilde{V}^{1/2}
. Combining our results above, we obtain the following.

Proposition H.6
.

Consider the statistic
\widetilde{V}
defined in (
345
). Under the null hypothesis,
\widetilde{V}
is an unbiased estimator for
\mathrm{Var}(T)
.

With this result in hand, it is possible to derive consistency of
\widetilde{V}
as an estimator of
\mathrm{Var}(T)
under certain regularity conditions. We omit the details.

H.1
Proof of Lemma
H.1

Recall that
B_{ijr}
is the Bernoulli random variable
B_{ijr}=Z_{ijr}+\Omega_{ij}
and satisfies
X_{ijr}=\sum_{r=1}^{N_{i}}B_{ijr}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle X_{ij}X_{ij^{\prime}}=\sum_{r,s}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}s}=\sum_{r}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}r}+\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}s}=0+\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}s}

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}X_{ij}X_{ij^{\prime}}=N_{i}(N_{i}-1)\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}},

and we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}}=\frac{X_{ij}X_{ij^{\prime}}}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}

is an unbiased estimator for
\Omega_{ij}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}
, as desired.
∎

H.2
Proof of Lemma
H.3

Note that

\displaystyle X_{ij}^{2}X_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(\sum_{r}B_{ijr}+\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ijr}B_{ijs}\big)\big(\sum_{r}B_{ij^{\prime}r}+\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ij^{\prime}r}B_{ij^{\prime}s}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}r}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}s}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq s}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijs}\sum_{r_{2}}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{2}}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq s}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{1}}B_{ij^{\prime}s}\sum_{r_{2}}B_{ijr_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\big(\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ijr}B_{ijs}\big)\big(\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ij^{\prime}r}B_{ij^{\prime}s}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}s}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq s}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijs}\sum_{r_{2}}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{2}}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq s}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{1}}B_{ij^{\prime}s}\sum_{r_{2}}B_{ijr_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\big(\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ijr}B_{ijs}\big)\big(\sum_{r\neq s}B_{ij^{\prime}r}B_{ij^{\prime}s}\big)

Since
B_{ijr}B_{ij^{\prime}r}=0
, note that

\displaystyle(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})(X_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}-X_{ij^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r_{1}\neq s_{1}}\sum_{r_{2}\neq s_{2}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijs_{1}}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{2}}B_{ij^{\prime}s_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r_{1},s_{1},r_{2},s_{2}\,dist.}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijs_{1}}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{2}}B_{ij^{\prime}s_{2}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})(X_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}-X_{ij^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r_{1},s_{1},r_{2},s_{2}\,dist.}\mathbb{E}\big[B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijs_{1}}B_{ij^{\prime}r_{2}}B_{ij^{\prime}s_{2}}\big]

\displaystyle=N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)\cdot\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}}=\frac{(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})(X_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}-X_{ij^{\prime}})}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)}

is an unbiased estimator for
\Omega_{ij}^{2}\Omega_{ij^{\prime}}^{2}
.

∎

H.3
Proof of Lemma
H.4

Recall that
B_{ijr}
is the Bernoulli random variable
B_{ijr}=Z_{ijr}+\Omega_{ij}
and satisfies
X_{ijr}=\sum_{r=1}^{N_{i}}B_{ijr}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle X_{ij}^{3}=\sum_{r}B_{ijr}+3\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}\neq r_{3}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}B_{ijr_{3}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}X_{ij}^{3}

\displaystyle=N_{i}\Omega_{ij}+3N_{i}(N_{i}-1)\Omega^{2}_{ij}+N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)\Omega_{ij}^{3}.

Unbiased estimators for
\Omega_{ij}
and
\Omega_{ij}^{2}
are

\displaystyle\frac{X_{ij}}{N_{i}}

\displaystyle\frac{X_{ij}^{2}}{N_{i}^{2}}-\frac{X_{ij}(N_{i}-X_{ij})}{N_{i}^{2}(N_{i}-1)}=\frac{1}{N_{i}(N_{i}-1)}\big(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij}\big),

respectively. Hence

\displaystyle X_{ij}^{3}-X_{ij}-3(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})=X_{ij}^{3}-3X_{ij}^{2}+2X_{ij}

is an unbiased estimator for
N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)\Omega_{ij}^{3}
, as desired.

∎

H.4
Proof of Lemma
H.5

Observe that

\displaystyle X_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}B_{ijr}^{4}+4\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr_{1}}^{3}B_{ijr_{2}}+6\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr_{1}}^{2}B_{ijr_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+3\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}\neq r_{3}}B_{ijr_{1}}^{2}B_{ijr_{2}}B_{ijr_{3}}+\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}\neq r_{3}\neq r_{4}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}B_{ijr_{3}}B_{ijr_{4}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r}B_{ijr}+10\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}+3\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}\neq r_{3}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}B_{ijr_{3}}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+\sum_{r_{1}\neq r_{2}\neq r_{3}\neq r_{4}}B_{ijr_{1}}B_{ijr_{2}}B_{ijr_{3}}B_{ijr_{4}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}X_{ij}^{4}

\displaystyle=N_{i}\Omega_{ij}+10N_{i}(N_{i}-1)\Omega_{ij}^{2}+3N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)\Omega_{ij}^{3}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.50012pt+N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)\Omega_{ij}^{4}.

Plugging in unbiased estimators for the first three terms, we have

\displaystyle X_{ij}^{4}-X_{ij}-10(X_{ij}^{2}-X_{ij})-3(X_{ij}^{3}-3X_{ij}^{2}+2X_{ij})=X_{ij}^{4}-3X_{ij}^{3}-X_{ij}^{2}+3X_{ij}

is an unbiased estimator for
N_{i}(N_{i}-1)(N_{i}-2)(N_{i}-3)
, as desired.
∎
